Founder real-device test 2026-08-17: the video PLAYS on iPhone, and SEEK works.** Both predicted harms did not occur. **Both things are true at once and neither cancels the other:** the mechanism is real and measured, *and* no user-visible harm was observed at this file size on a real device — a browser that simply fetches the whole 2.22 MB file is entirely consistent with seeking working. ⚠️ **Evidence boundary, stated honestly: ONE 2.3 MB file, ONE device, ONE network.** That does **not** clear the gap for larger files or poor mobile networks, where fetching the whole file before playback is exactly the failure ranges exist to prevent. **CAUSE, CORRECTED:** `fastapi==0.111.0` pins Starlette ≈0.37, and **Range support landed in Starlette 0.39.0 — NOT 0.45** (0.38.6 has no range markers; 0.39.0 has `accept-ranges`, `206` and `_parse_range_header`, established by downloading the wheels and reading `responses.py` out of each). **The minimum bump is therefore `fastapi 0.115.2`, not 0.115.7** — 0.115.1 caps at `starlette<0.39.0`. **NOT FIXED — filed as `[OTHER][P2]` TECH_DEBT** with the fix path and its verification cost. ⚠️ Also observed, pre-existing and not from this deploy: **unknown paths under `/media/` return `200` + `index.html` rather than `404`** (proved with a deliberately-nonexistent control path) — an SPA fallback, which means a naive status-code check cannot prove a file is absent. **D-B4.1-1 IS DISCHARGED**; the landing returns to normal push/deploy discipline. ❌ ~~**CURRENT STATE: prod = fly 232 (hotfix) · landing in LOCAL ITERATION MODE (D-B4.1-1, founder-ratified 2026-08-12).**~~ **fly 232 = hero-spotlight horizontal-overflow hotfix**, readback-verified `revision` = **`2c1e5db772c692038fce3d6fca9dbd9f08f8d7b3`**. Defect: the page scrolled sideways with the hero text clipped left and blank space right — **founder-confirmed on a real phone**, live since B4. Cause: `.hero-spotlight` is 190% of the composer form (max-w-2xl), i.e. ~1277px centred on the viewport, so it overran the right edge on **every viewport under ~1277px** — phones, tablets and any non-maximised laptop, not just phones. Fix: `overflow-x-clip` on the first-viewport wrapper — **clip, never `hidden`** (hidden would create a scroll container and force the other axis to `auto`, and the ScrollTrigger pin is live in this build), and chosen over resizing the blob so desktop geometry is pixel-identical by construction. **fly 234 (2026-08-17) — ✅ SHIPPED: Deletion-feature C pieces #2 + #1. NOT a docs-only deploy.** Pushed `074a998..2c533bf`; **`/health` `revision` = `2c533bfa720e6908fd7b6819befcd57032f0512d` = pushed HEAD, EXACT full-string match** (recorded in full precisely because a prefix match is not a match). fly **v234** complete, both machines running. **4 commits separated prod from HEAD and TWO carried product code** — `9439699` (`api/server.py` + the new `api/services/deletion_service.py` + `scripts/deletion_dryrun.py`) and `050a0d7` (`deletion_service.py`) — verified by classifying each commit's file list before pushing, not by assuming. **WHAT SHIPPED:** the **`_hard_delete_user()` helper**, which is in the image and **reachable from no route**, and the **`POST /api/webhooks/clerk` surface**, which is **non-destructive by construction** — it verifies a Standard-Webhooks signature and writes `webhook_events` only. **READINESS, run BEFORE the push:** `tsc` 0 · **pytest 291 passed / 28 skipped — exact match to the recorded baseline** · build clean · landing probe **63/63** · mp4 box probe **faststart YES, audio tracks 0** · **`out/media` exactly the 2 shipped files** (B4.5 D1 guarantee holds) · export 6.97 MB. **POST-DEPLOY VERIFICATION.** **(a)** `POST /api/webhooks/clerk` → **`500 {"detail":"Webhook not configured"}`**. That is the designed response with `CLERK_WEBHOOK_SECRET` unset, and it is the useful one: it **proves routing and reachability independently of signature verification**, because the handler returns before it reads any header. Message is generic — **no `str(e)`, no traceback (Rule 5)**. ⚠️ **SUPERSEDED AT fly 235 — with the secret set, this same request now returns `401 {"detail":"Missing signature headers"}`. The 500 is retained as the accurate fly-234 observation it was, marked so nobody curls the endpoint today and concludes the doc is wrong.** **(b)** **NON-DESTRUCTIVE CONFIRMED IN THE RUNNING APP, not just in tests:** `/openapi.json` enumerates **36 routes**; **none has a deletion-shaped path and no `operationId` contains delete/erase/purge**. The one `hard_delete` string in the whole spec is the **`description` of `POST /api/webhooks/clerk` — the handler's own docstring sentence saying it does NOT call the helper.** (My first automated check flagged that as a FAIL; it was the check being wrong — the third time prose-in-a-docstring has satisfied a substring search in this repo.) **(c)** App booted clean with the new module and route: `/health` 200, and `/api/user/status`, `/api/history`, `/api/research`, `/api/explain`, `/api/verify` all answer **403 `Missing token`** — registered, reachable, auth guard running. ⚠️ **Stated honestly: that is REACHABILITY, not streaming.** End-to-end SSE was **not** exercised, because doing so on prod means a real credit-costing LLM call; and since no dependency changed this deploy, a startup regression is what the smoke was for and it is ruled out. **A second FAIL in my script — `HEAD /research` → 404 — is the KNOWN, already-filed GET/HEAD asymmetry** (`@app.get("/{path:path}")` is GET-only; FastAPI does not auto-add HEAD, so HEAD falls through to the StaticFiles mount). `GET /research` = 200. Pre-existing, not from this deploy. ✅ **THE CLERK PATH IS VERIFIED — corrected IN PLACE 2026-08-17, replacing this entry's original "SPEC-CORRECT BUT UNVERIFIED" assertion rather than appending to it.** *(The original read: the secret is not set, no real delivery has ever reached the endpoint, the signature path cannot be exercised, "do not record it as working." Every clause of that was true when written and every clause is now false. It is replaced, not struck, because a struck-through claim still reads as a claim — and this repo's own record says why: the Rule 12 incident (a rule true when written, false for two months, still being followed), two false assertions shipped into this very work line's docs (the Starlette 0.45 figure, the iPhone playback prediction), and a stale SOP copy that nearly misdirected a Step-7 reference.)* **THE MEASURED FACTS, founder-run:** a **real Clerk delivery** via the Dashboard Testing tab, event **`user.deleted`**, **2026-08-17 14:28 → HTTP 200, Succeeded**. A **Replay of the SAME message at 14:31 → HTTP 200 `{"status":"already_processed"}`**, and the `svix-id` was **byte-identical across both attempts — `msg_3I221njd6SRz2LKs56Aqpan5esZ`**. 🔑 **That byte-identity is what makes the replay meaningful: had Replay minted a fresh id, the idempotency path would not have been exercised at all** — it would merely have inserted a second row. **INDEPENDENT CONFIRMATION, not the app's own word:** `webhook_events` holds **EXACTLY ONE row** for `clerk_msg_3I221njd6SRz2LKs56Aqpan5esZ`, `event_type` `user.deleted`. **Two deliveries, one row.** The `already_processed` response is the application reporting on itself; the single row is evidence independent of it. 🔑 **THE HEADER QUESTION IS ANSWERED, and this is the fact a future reader cannot re-derive locally: Clerk sends `svix-id` / `svix-signature` / `svix-timestamp`.** The Dodo verification block this handler was hand-copied from reads **`webhook-*` only** (`api/server.py:2037-2039`). **So the `svix-*` half of `_hdr()` (`:2222-2224`) is LOAD-BEARING — without it every real Clerk delivery would have 401'd at `:2226-2227`.** *(Precision the baton's phrasing did not carry, corrected here: it is the **`svix-*` half** that was necessary. The `webhook-*` half is the Standard-Webhooks fallback and is now known to be **unexercised by Clerk** — retained as spec-conformance, not as a proven path.)* **Signature format observed: `v1,<base64>` — Standard Webhooks, confirming the reuse decision; no `svix` package is needed or installed.** **CONFIG:** `CLERK_WEBHOOK_SECRET` set via `fly secrets` 2026-08-17, producing **fly 235**. ⚠️ **THAT IS A ROLLING RESTART, NOT A REBUILD — `/health` `revision` stayed `2c533bf`, which is CORRECT and must not be read as a failed rollout.** Said explicitly because the next person to set a secret will check the revision and wonder. **The 500→401 transition was used deliberately as the discriminator between "is the secret present" and "does signature verification work":** an unsigned POST now returns **`401 {"detail":"Missing signature headers"}`** — generic, no `str(e)` (Rule 5). ⚠️ **DO NOT OVERSTATE THE SCOPE. What is proven is the SURFACE: signature verification, header handling, idempotency, and the `webhook_events` write. NOTHING about deletion automation was tested, because the handler performs none.** *(Re-verified independently while writing this, read-only against prod: `/health` `revision` = `2c533bfa720e6908fd7b6819befcd57032f0512d`, and an unsigned `POST /api/webhooks/clerk` → `401 {"detail":"Missing signature headers"}` — no longer the 500, which is itself proof the secret is set.)* ⚠️ **Gate 6's remaining prod re-check rows 1, 2 and 5+6 were NOT attempted — founder-run visual checks.** ⚠️ **NEW, flagged not fixed: `/openapi.json` is publicly reachable and publishes every route plus handler DOCSTRINGS** — the Clerk handler's internal design notes (the `_hard_delete_user` sentence, `user_usage`, the TOCTOU reasoning, ~~the secret's env-var name~~) are readable at `https://vela.an-tho.com/openapi.json`. ⚠️ **ONE CLAUSE OF MY OWN WAS FALSE AND IS STRUCK: `CLERK_WEBHOOK_SECRET` was NEVER in the published schema — measured 2026-08-18 by generating the schema and counting, 0 occurrences before the fix and 0 after. The docstring never named it. I asserted it from memory of the handler rather than from the artifact, which is the exact failure this work line keeps hitting.** ✅ **THE REST WAS TRUE AND IS NOW FIXED — see the fly-235 code entry below: the docstring is public, the rationale moved to `#` comments, and the published description went 1367 → 385 chars with every internal string measured gone.** Not a vulnerability, but internal detail on a public surface, and it is what made check (b) misfire.

**Rule-7 classification + citation sweep + two shipped-doc corrections (2026-08-17). Docs-only; NOT pushed, NOT deployed; no code changed, so `pytest` stays 291 passed / 28 skipped.** **A — RULE 7 CLASSIFIED, RULE STILL NOT WRITTEN (read-only, as instructed).** 🔑 **The census found a THIRD property of `_safe_db_write` that neither the ruling nor the baton names, and it is the one that actually separates the sites: THE HELPER IS INSERT-ONLY.** It is `for rec in records: db.add(rec)` + `commit()`, so it can only ever be *called with new ORM objects*. It cannot express a bulk `query().delete()`, a bulk `query().update()`, an `on_conflict_do_update()` UPSERT, or raw `text()` SQL. **This is a capability limit, not a choice — and it is ALREADY DOCUMENTED IN-REPO**, at `api/server.py:2380-2383`, where `user_context_hash_upsert` records a Rule 7 deviation *"because the helper is INSERT-only (db.add + commit) and cannot express atomic UPSERT semantics."* **Testing the baton's three candidate principles against the real sites: "background tasks have no caller to read a return value" DOES NOT HOLD** — all five background sites swallow-and-log exactly as `_safe_db_write` would, so they are avoiding neither property; what distinguishes `_cleanup_old_records` is that it is a bulk DELETE the helper cannot express. **"Idempotency handlers must distinguish duplicate from failure" HOLDS but explains exactly ONE site** (`clerk_webhook`, and only because it was written that way deliberately — the other two webhooks make no such distinction). **"Multi-statement routines need one transaction" HOLDS for the deletion routine** (ratified exception (a)) **but is not what separates these 25** — a `_safe_db_write` call commits the whole session including dirty objects, so it would not actually have split them. **RECOMMENDED SHAPE: state the PRINCIPLE with a CHECK, never a named list** — a list of endpoints rots on the next endpoint exactly as five line-number citations have now rotted. The honest principle is roughly *"`_safe_db_write` is the required path for INSERTING new records on a request path; any site whose DML it cannot express, or which must distinguish specific exception types, states why in a comment at the call site"* — which is the form `user_context_hash_upsert` already follows unprompted. **RESIDUAL DRIFT IS REAL AND SMALL:** plain-INSERT sites in `server.py` that *could* use the helper and do not — `explain_identify_feedback` (1630), `create_feedback` (1732), `dodo_webhook` (2091). Not defects (each wraps in `try/except` and returns a proper error, arguably better than swallow-and-return-`False`), but unexplained variance. **⚠️ SCOPE GAP IS NINE SITES, NOT ONE:** Rule 7 says *"in server.py"* and therefore does not reach `usage_service.py` (7) or `cost_tracker.py` (2) at all — including the credit-deduction commits, which are the most consequence-bearing writes in the app. ❌ **CORRECTION TO MY OWN EARLIER NUMBER: there are 11 real `_safe_db_write` call sites, not 12** — my previous count included two prose mentions of the name. The in-repo comment at `:2383` also says "all 12", so it carries the same off-by-one. **B — CITATION SWEEP.** **SOP: 4 of 4 converted to symbols** — and 🔴 **THREE OF THE FOUR WERE ALREADY STALE, pointing at unrelated code**: `server.py:1448` (cited for the forward `user_id → email` lookup) now lands inside `verify_drug_interaction` computing a risk level; `server.py:1819` (cited for `cancel_subscription`) now lands inside `get_user_history`; `server.py:1605-1606` (cited for the Dodo `plan_type` flip) now lands on **blank lines** inside `explain_report`. Resolved to `create_dodo_checkout()`, `cancel_subscription()` and `dodo_webhook()` respectively, each verified to exist at HEAD. **BACKLOG: 34 citations found, 3 converted, 31 deliberately NOT converted.** Only the three where the prose **already named the symbol** were touched, so dropping the number asserted nothing new. The rest are left because **at least 7 already land on blank lines or comments** — meaning today's enclosing symbol is *not* the symbol the claim meant, and converting a wrong number into a confident wrong symbol name would make it worse, not better (a stale number looks suspicious; a stale symbol reads as verified). **`BACKLOG:1399` is a live demonstration: it cites `api/server.py:2211`, which now lands inside the `clerk_webhook` I added last baton — a citation that rotted because of my own insert.** Recommendation: cite symbols going forward, and repair the 31 opportunistically when their entries are next touched and their context is at hand. **C — TWO FALSE ASSERTIONS WE SHIPPED INTO OUR OWN DOCS, CORRECTED IN BOTH PLACES** (fly-233 STATE header + the Gate 6 block, which duplicate each other). **C1 VERSION:** Range support landed in **Starlette 0.39.0, NOT 0.45** — 0.38.6 has no range markers, 0.39.0 has `accept-ranges`, `206` and `_parse_range_header` (established by downloading the wheels and reading `responses.py` out of each). **Consequence: the minimum bump is `fastapi 0.115.2`, not 0.115.7** (0.115.1 caps at `starlette<0.39.0`). **C2 PREDICTION REFUTED:** *"the demo will very likely NOT play on iPhone"* is **wrong** — founder real-device test 2026-08-17: **it PLAYS and SEEK WORKS.** Rewritten to hold both truths, because both are true: the mechanism is real and still measured (`Range: bytes=0-1023` → 200, full 2,325,186 bytes, no `Accept-Ranges`/`Content-Range`), **and** no user-visible harm was observed — a browser fetching the whole file is *consistent* with seeking working. ⚠️ **Evidence boundary stated in both places: ONE 2.3 MB file, ONE device, ONE network — this does NOT clear the gap for larger files or poor mobile networks.** **C3 GATE 6 ROW 8 IS COMPLETE — PASSED BY REAL DEVICE**, with the original DevTools-emulation caveat **preserved as history rather than deleted**. **The prod re-check is now rows 1, 2 and 5+6.** **C4 FILED AS TECH_DEBT, NOT AS A DEFECT:** `[OTHER][P2]` for the range gap — class *correctness gap with no observed user harm* — carrying the fix path (**`fastapi 0.115.2` PLUS an explicit `starlette==` pin, because the widened `<0.41.0` range still contains the rangeless 0.37/0.38 line and an unpinned rebuild could silently restore the bug with nothing in `/health` to reveal it**) and the verification cost (SSE + both payment webhooks have zero automated coverage). 🔴 **Per Rule 20's own escape clause the entry SAYS the artifact is not retained and why** — every number came from session-scratchpad probes against a live host and a package index, so re-running produces a new snapshot, not the one the finding rests on; it must not be cited as reproducible without re-measuring. Plus `[OTHER][P3]` for the missing `Cache-Control` on all static assets (checked first — it was not already filed). **Nav counts RECOUNTED from the file rather than incremented: OTHER 67→69, total 124→126 — the recount CONFIRMED the table (my +2 this baton is the only delta; no hidden drift this time).**

**Deletion-feature C — FOLLOW-UP to `9439699` (2026-08-17). 4 of 5 ratified items applied; ITEM 2 IS BLOCKED AND WAS NOT WRITTEN. Still NOT pushed, NOT deployed; nothing ran against prod.** **① SIGNATURE STANDS + docs synced:** `BACKLOG.md:1505` and the SOP's closing line now carry the real `_hard_delete_user(db, user_id, *, created_by_hash)` and its module path, with the FLAG #2 reason for the required keyword-only param and the Step-3-only scope. The SOP's `_hash_created_by()` citation was corrected — ⚠️ **to `server.py:2934-2938`, NOT the 2818-2822 the baton gave**: my own Clerk-webhook insert in `9439699` (+116 lines) moved it after that number was measured. The formula is untouched. *Second time in two batons a doc line-number citation has gone stale; line numbers in prose are load-bearing here and rot on every insert.* **② RULE 7 — 🔴 NOT WRITTEN, BLOCKED TWICE OVER, DELIBERATELY LEFT ALONE.** (i) The baton says the founder-approved replacement text is "reproduced in the baton hand-off" — **it is not present anywhere in the baton**, and the instruction is explicit that a governance rule must not be paraphrased, so it was not. (ii) **More seriously, the ruling's own premise is refuted by the verification it asked for.** The enumeration is "the three webhook handlers commit directly". Measured: **25 direct `.commit()` sites under `api/`** — 1 is `_safe_db_write` itself (`server.py:172`), **4** are the three webhooks (`2018` lemonsqueezy, **`2091` AND `2174` dodo — two sites, not one**, `2284` clerk), and **20 are neither**: 4 background tasks (`194`, `532`, `565`, `596`, `634` — five, in `_cleanup_old_records` / judge / direction-check / retrieval-refusal / source-weight-shadow), **7 decorated endpoints** (`1630` `/api/explain/feedback`, `1732` `/api/feedback`, `2441` `/api/user/context/hash`, `2653` `/q/{share_id}`, `2825` `/explore/{slug}`, `3189` `/api/share/{share_id}/revoke`), and **9 in `api/services/`** (`usage_service` ×7, `cost_tracker` ×2) which Rule 7's "in server.py" wording does not even reach. **An exception list naming 4 of 25 sites, enumerated as "three webhooks", would have been exactly the assumed-not-verified failure the rewrite exists to end.** Needs a founder ruling on the real scope before any text is written. **③ NON-DESTRUCTIVE PROPERTY PINNED:** `test_clerk_webhook_stays_non_destructive` isolates the handler and **strips docstrings before matching** — the handler's own prose says it does not call `_hard_delete_user`, so a naive substring search passes for the wrong reason. Its docstring states the job explicitly: **the guard is NOT there to block wiring deletion to the webhook later — it is there so whoever does it must DELETE THIS TEST DELIBERATELY, in the same commit, rather than let the surface drift from non-destructive to destructive as a side effect. Removing the test IS the decision point.** Includes a positive control so the assertions cannot pass on an empty slice. **④ `created_by_hash` SHAPE ASSERTED, RESIDUAL GAP IN WRITING:** `ValueError` (not `assert` — asserts vanish under `python -O` and this is a data-destruction guard) on anything that is not 16 lowercase hex, raised **before any statement runs**, so a malformed call deletes nothing. Shape verified against the real `_hash_created_by`. 🔴 **The docstring says plainly what it CANNOT catch: a WELL-FORMED hash computed with the WRONG SALT passes every check, matches zero `shared_query` rows, and leaves authorship silently intact — SOP FLAG #2 exactly. That gap cannot be closed inside the helper (the salt lives in the caller's environment); the only defence is the SOP's Step-2 non-zero-count check, which is a HUMAN step.** The assertion must not be read as FLAG #2 being handled. **⑤ ANSWER, HONESTLY: YES — `9439699` DID update STATE.md** (2 insertions). It was done; my report of that commit simply never said so. That was an omission in the reporting, not in the work. **STATE FOR THIS SEGMENT:** both pieces built and committed (`9439699` + this follow-up), **NOT pushed, NOT deployed**. Harness on **DEV only**: **`--impl sql` 14/14 and `--impl python` 14/14, both rolled back, zero residue** — re-run after the shape assertion landed and still 14/14 both ways. 🔴 ~~**THE CLERK PATH REMAINS SPEC-CORRECT BUT UNVERIFIED: no real Clerk delivery has ever reached the endpoint, and NO LOCAL METHOD CAN CHANGE THAT — verification requires a deploy plus a genuine delivery from Clerk. It is recorded as unverified, not as working.**~~ ✅ **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-17 — VERIFIED by a real Clerk delivery + replay; see the fly-234 entry above for the measured facts.** *(The prediction this sentence made was exactly right and is left visible for that reason: no local method could change it, and what changed it was precisely a deploy plus a genuine delivery. Marked rather than replaced because, unlike the fly-234 assertion, this one is a dated statement about local methods that remains true as written — it is the "REMAINS UNVERIFIED" status that expired, not the reasoning.)* **The Step-7 counts are split — `user_usage_total` 1→1 (RETAINED) vs `user_usage_active` 1→0 (FROZEN) — because a single `user_usage: 1→0` line in the operator log would read as "the billing row was deleted", which is the exact misreading design E's freeze exists to prevent.** **`pytest` 291 passed / 28 skipped** (283 → 289 at `9439699` → **291** here, +2 from items ③/④). **ALL 13 MUTATIONS CAUGHT** (the original 8 plus N9 webhook-calls-deletion, N10 webhook-touches-user_usage, N11 shape-check-removed, N12 shape-regex-weakened, N13 user_id-check-removed). No PRD marker moved, no BACKLOG entry removed — nothing shipped.

**Deletion-feature C — PIECES #2 + #1 BUILT LOCALLY, NOT PUSHED, NOT DEPLOYED (2026-08-17).** Nothing ran against prod; the only DB touched was the **dev** branch, inside rollback-only transactions. **PIECE #2 — `_hard_delete_user()` lives at `api/services/deletion_service.py`** (a NEW module, not `server.py`: importing `api.server` runs `sentry_sdk.init`, builds the FastAPI app, adds middleware and calls `Path("static/og").mkdir()` at import time — a deletion helper must not drag that in). **It is unreachable from every route — verified, not asserted:** the only mention of it anywhere under `api/` outside its own module is a *docstring sentence in the Clerk webhook saying it is not called.* **Contract, all four points in the docstring:** does NOT commit (caller owns the transaction — required by the ratified Rule 7 exception AND by the harness, whose zero-residue guarantee IS the outer `db.rollback()`); does NOT swallow exceptions; **implements SOP Step 3 ONLY — explicitly NOT Step 1 (cancel Dodo) and NOT Step 5 (Clerk reconcile), with the misuse spelled out in the docstring in those words: a caller that invokes this and nothing else would erase the user's data while they keep getting billed**; returns per-table before/after counts for SOP Step 7. Behaviour is verbatim from the SOP at HEAD — 5 hard deletes, `api_cost_log` SET NULL (rows retained), `user_usage` FREEZE via `deleted_at` (**not** pseudonymize; FLAG #1 retired at the SOP's own L192-193), `shared_query.created_by` SET NULL (page kept). Implemented in **ORM calls, deliberately not by re-executing the SOP's SQL strings** — a helper that replayed them would make the equivalence test below a tautology. 🔴 **THE `created_by` HASH QUESTION WAS REPORTED, NOT DECIDED SILENTLY — and it forced a signature deviation the founder should rule on.** The baton specifies `_hard_delete_user(db, user_id)`; what is implemented is **`_hard_delete_user(db, user_id, *, created_by_hash)`** — required, keyword-only, no default. Reasons: `_hash_created_by()` lives at **`server.py:2818-2822`** and computing internally would mean the heavy import above; duplicating the formula would create a third copy (SOP, harness, service) to keep in sync; and an optional/defaulted parameter makes SOP **FLAG #2** — wrong-or-missing salt matches **zero** rows and silently leaves authorship links intact — the *easy* path. Required + keyword-only makes forgetting it a `TypeError` at the call site instead of a silent no-op. **Trivially reversible if the founder prefers the literal two-arg signature.** **HARNESS:** `scripts/deletion_dryrun.py` gains `--impl sql|python`, **default `sql` so today's behaviour is unchanged**. Every guard preserved byte-for-byte — verified by diffing: the `DEV_SUBSTR` allow-list, the `DELETION_DRYRUN_ALLOW` gate, `finally: db.rollback()`, and `--plan` as the no-arg default that opens no connection (the only diff line matching a guard pattern is a new help string). **BOTH PATHS RAN ON DEV AND BOTH PASSED 14/14, rolled back, zero residue** — `impl=sql` 14/14 and `impl=python` 14/14, the same asserts, which is the equivalence evidence: the SQL is the lawyer-confirmed artifact, so the helper is proven not to have diverged from the legally signed-off behaviour. The Python run also printed the Step-7 counts and they show the design-E distinction exactly: **`user_usage_total` 1→1 (RETAINED) while `user_usage_active` 1→0 (FROZEN)** — reported as two numbers on purpose, because a single `user_usage: 1→0` line in the operator log would read as "the billing row was deleted", the precise misreading the freeze exists to prevent. **PYTEST — `tests/test_deletion_coverage.py`, 6 DB-free guards** (no connection: `create_engine` is lazy). The load-bearing one asserts the model's 12 tables **partition exactly** into the 8 the routine touches + the 4 FLAG #3 declares out of scope, with both sets **derived** (`Base.metadata.tables` and the service's own `DELETION_TABLES`/`OUT_OF_SCOPE_TABLES`), never hand-typed — a hand-typed list rots the moment either side changes, which is the failure it exists to prevent. Business rule (Rule 17): **a new table holding user data is added and the deletion routine silently fails to cover it — a compliance breach against the deployed Policy §4 that nothing else would catch; the dry-run would still pass 14/14 because it only asserts on tables it already knows.** Plus the no-commit contract via a mock session, with a negative control pinning 5 deletes / 3 updates / 18 counts so "no commit" cannot be satisfied by "no work". **ALL 8 MUTATIONS CAUGHT**, including **N8 — a new user-data table appended to `sql_models.py`, the real scenario.** **PIECE #1 — `POST /api/webhooks/clerk` (`api/server.py:2181`), NON-DESTRUCTIVE.** Writes `webhook_events` **only**; **verified by inspecting the handler body with its docstring stripped — `_hard_delete_user`, `UserUsage` and `user_usage` are absent from the code and appear only in prose.** No `deleted_at`, no `.delete(`/`.update(`. **Signature reuses the Standard Webhooks scheme `dodo_webhook` already hand-implements — no `svix` package pinned, installed or needed** — and reads **both** `svix-*` and `webhook-*` header spellings, svix first. ⚠️ ~~**THE CLERK PATH IS SPEC-CORRECT BUT EXPLICITLY UNVERIFIED: no real Clerk delivery has ever reached it.** Do not read "spec-correct" as "working" (Rule 18).~~ ✅ **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-17 — VERIFIED (real delivery + replay + one `webhook_events` row); the fly-234 entry above carries the measured facts. `CLERK_WEBHOOK_SECRET` is now SET on prod (fly 235).** ⚠️ **The SURFACE is verified; the AUTOMATION still does not exist.** **Idempotency fixes the class instead of propagating it:** insert-first + `except IntegrityError → already_processed`, so the PK does the de-duplication and there is no check-to-write window. The two existing webhooks keep the TOCTOU defect (500 instead of `already_processed` on a concurrent duplicate) — **filed as a new `[OTHER][P2]` TECH_DEBT entry, not fixed here.** Event id prefixed **`clerk_`** (Dodo prefixes, LemonSqueezy stores a bare uuid — an unprefixed third provider is a PK-collision risk). **No migration — verified independently against `sql_models.py`, not inherited from BACKLOG:** `webhook_events.event_id` is VARCHAR **NOT NULL, `PK cols: ['event_id']`**. ~~Needs the new env var `CLERK_WEBHOOK_SECRET`.~~ ✅ **SET on prod 2026-08-17 → fly 235 (a rolling restart, so `/health` `revision` correctly stayed `2c533bf`).** Error semantics mirror the existing two: **401** bad/missing signature or stale timestamp (±300s replay window), **500** missing/malformed secret, generic messages only — **no `str(e)` anywhere (Rule 5), no `print()` (Rule 4)**. **TECH_DEBT recorded in the fly-222 split, deliberately:** *the SURFACE now exists, the AUTOMATION does not* — a Clerk-side deletion still produces **no change** to Vela's database and SOP Step 5 remains a human step. **It is NOT written as "webhook gap closed."** **RULE 7:** ratified exception (a) honoured — the deletion routine is one transaction and does not use `_safe_db_write()`. ⚠️ **A SECOND, NARROWER deviation is FLAGGED FOR RATIFICATION, not self-granted:** the Clerk handler's `except IntegrityError` cannot be expressed through `_safe_db_write()`, which catches everything and returns `False` — that would report a **database outage as `already_processed`**. Note both existing webhooks already commit directly without it, so this surface has never used the helper; one ruling covering all three would be cleaner than three ad-hoc calls. **RULE 16 confirmed NON-APPLICABLE, checked not assumed: 0 frontend files changed**, no new user-visible strings — the webhook answers Clerk's servers and the helper has no UI. **VERIFIED:** `pytest` **289 passed / 28 skipped** (283 → +6). No push, no deploy, no PRD marker moved, no BACKLOG entry removed — **nothing shipped.** ⚠️ **DRIFT FOUND, NOT FIXED:** the SOP at **L82** cites `_hash_created_by()` as `server.py:2370-2374`; it is actually at **`server.py:2818-2822`** (the algorithm itself is unchanged and still matches the SOP and the harness). Also unchanged from the recon: `sse-starlette==2.1.0` is pinned but never imported; HEAD/GET disagree on extensionless pages; the SPA fallback returns 200+`index.html` for unknown `/api/*`; `tests/test_webhook_cancel.py` still `sys.exit(1)`s at import and is excluded from every run, so the payment surface still has no executable coverage.

**B4.5 (local, 2026-08-14) — answer-pane auto-scroll fix (PRODUCT) + near-black panel + gradient removal + the deferred B4.4 hygiene.**
**(A) ANSWER PANE — the founder-reported defect is REAL, RESEARCH-ONLY, and was ONE line.** Investigation first: the entire repo contains **exactly one** answer-pane scroll site, `pages/research.tsx:258`, a `useEffect` on `[answer]` doing `scrollTop = scrollHeight` on **every token** — so at end-of-stream the user is parked at the BOTTOM of a long answer and must scroll UP to the Summary. **Verify and Explain do NOT share a component and do NOT duplicate it** — they are separate pages with **zero** scroll machinery (`git grep scrollTop|scrollIntoView|scrollTo` over `pages/`+`components/` returns only this site and the landing's chevron). Explain streams over SSE too but never auto-follows, so it cannot exhibit the defect; Verify does not even use `fetchEventSource`. **One fix, one surface, and nothing to carry across** — stated because Rule 19 would otherwise demand the sweep. **The completion signal is the `loading` true→false EDGE**, tracked with a `wasLoadingRef`: `loading` goes false in exactly two places, both genuine end-of-stream (`data.type === 'done'` at `research.tsx:438-439` and `onclose()` at :458) — **not a timeout, not the first gap in tokens.** ✅ **ORDERING CHECKED, NOT ASSUMED:** `loading` is *also* what swaps the raw stream for the composed render (`const sections = !loading ? parseResearchSections(...)`, plus the `ProvenanceLine`/`FallbackBanner` row, all gated on `!loading`), so both come from the SAME state change and React commits the composed DOM **before** the effect runs — the scroll lands on the finished Summary, not ahead of it. Scrolling to `0` is height-independent, so the re-render changing `scrollHeight` cannot invalidate it either. **DECISION ON FIGHTING THE USER — always snap, and the reason is that there is no user position to preserve:** the follow effect overwrites `scrollTop` on *every chunk*, so a manual scroll during generation is destroyed within one token; "the user deliberately scrolled away" is a state that **cannot survive to completion** in the current design. Given that, top-of-answer beats bottom-of-answer unconditionally. ⚠️ **Flagged, NOT changed:** making the follow YIELD to a manual scroll is a separate and larger change and was not in this baton — say so if it should be. Reduced motion gets `behavior:'auto'` (instant), everyone else `'smooth'`. **Regression test: `tests/test_research_answer_scroll.py`, 6 tests — and its scope is stated honestly in its own docstring.** They are SOURCE assertions, not behavioural: this repo has **no JS test runner**, and the completion path needs a live SSE stream, the FastAPI backend and a real credit-costing query to reach at all. What they do pin is the structure, including the one premise that can break **silently from a distance** — `test_composed_render_is_still_gated_on_loading`, because if the composed render is ever driven by a different trigger the ordering guarantee dissolves with nothing else complaining. Comments are stripped before matching (the fix is documented by a ~30-line comment naming every identifier asserted, so without stripping, deleting the whole implementation would leave the suite green). **Mutation-tested against the real file: 5/5 caught** (scroll deleted, edge guard replaced by the naive `if (loading) return`, reduced-motion ignored, stream-following removed, composed render un-gated). ⚠️ **END-TO-END NOT RUN** — a real query on `/research` is the remaining check and it is the founder's, since it needs the backend and spends credits.
**(B) NEAR-BLACK PANEL — founder-ratified, recorded as a KNOWING aesthetic decision, not a regression.** `--color-panel` **62 48 40 → 18 18 18**. This reverses B4.3's warm brown-charcoal deliberately: the founder has seen #3E3028 rendered and chose near-black anyway. What it trades away is kept in the CSS comment as the record — **the paper→panel lightness cliff returns to ~91 points (98.0% → 7.1%), WIDER than the 89 of the original ink panel**, and at **S=0%** the panel now sits **outside** the paper/hero hue family (H24°), which is the exact kinship B4.3 was built to create. **CONTRAST MEASURED IN-BROWSER from computed styles (not hand-estimated — and my hand computation agreed to within 0.01, checked precisely because the B4.1d session's estimate was wrong by 0.1):** headline/body paper@1.0 **17.95:1** (was 12.13) · sub paper@0.70 **9.08:1** (was 6.83) · CTA pill label **17.95:1** (was 12.13) · media hairline paper@0.15 **1.52:1** (decorative, not text). Every text value clears AA normal with far more margin than #3E3028 had, and with the gradient gone the panel's **weakest text is now the sub at 9.08:1**. **HAIRLINE REVERTED /20 → /15**: the B4.3 raise was compensation for the panel *lightening*, and that cause is gone — near-black is **darker than the ink the /15 hairline originally sat on** (1.52:1 now vs 1.55:1 historically = the original look restored; /20 would read 1.82:1). One-token flip if the founder disagrees.

Reported, not silently swapped; the founder's accepted fallback (plain Option A, 21.30→38.60, 17.3s, query-time visible ~3.3s) is **one re-encode away and needs ZERO Phase-2 change** — the wiring is invariant to which cut wins. **ENCODE:** ONE pass from the ORIGINAL via `filter_complex` `trim`+`concat` (no intermediate files, never an encode of an encode); `-pix_fmt yuv420p` added for browser compat, which the baton did not specify. **OUTPUT VERIFIED TWO WAYS** — box parse `ftyp → moov → free → mdat` = **faststart YES**, handlers `vide, mdir` only = **audio tracks 0**; ffprobe **1440×812 High yuv420p 30 fps, 396 frames, 13.200s, 2,013,596 B (1.92 MB, 1.22 Mbps)** = **32% of the 6 MB budget, so CRF 23 was not needed**. The box parser is committed as **`tests/probes/b43_mp4_boxes.mjs`** + result JSON (**Rule 20** — B4.3's parse left no artifact and was unverifiable by anyone else); **NEGATIVE-CONTROLLED**: run against the original it reports faststart NO + 1 audio track and exits 1, so it can fail. **Poster** taken from the **ORIGINAL at t=38.00** (= 12.60s into the cut), **not from the encode**, to avoid compounding h264 artifacts into a JPEG — 1440×812, 96,918 B (94.6 KB). Carried across as **`width=1440 height=812` + an explicit `aspect-ratio:1440 / 812`** so the zero-CLS guarantee does not depend on UA behaviour for `<video>`. **DEVIATION (stated): NO `autoplay` attribute.** The attribute starts playback as soon as the element parses, which would leave "never plays off-screen" resting on a browser heuristic (Chrome defers off-screen muted autoplay; Firefox does not); playback is IntersectionObserver-driven only, and the probe pins the attribute's ABSENCE. **VERIFIED LIVE (hidden-window guard fired FIRST — `visibilityState: hidden`):** panel **767px = 92.0vh at 1024 / 1050 / 1280px width — height CONSTANT**, media **52.6% of panel** (above the 40% floor), video ratio **1.7734 = exactly 1440/812, no squash**, hOverflow 0. ### Task-A Answer-Quality QA + B2C/B2B decision (2026-06-15)

**Task-A medical-answer-quality QA is COMPLETE** (measurement only — NO product change, NO route decision made). Three layers run this session; all artifacts in `tests/results/` (gitignored):

- **Stage 1 — full 127-case golden eval** (`golden_results_20260613_144748.json`): raw 74.8% but heavily contaminated — 22/24 FAILs were harness/guard artifacts (v179 UI-language harness staleness ×6; guard over-block of colloquial symptom queries ×11; non-Latin-script identify ×4; transient ×1). Guard 100%, PHI 100%, Explain `no_fabricated_citations` hard-floor 0 violations, Explain disclaimer 17/17. **2 REAL content gaps**: R10 (digoxin toxicity omits nausea/vomiting + visual disturbances) + the persistent canonical-term under-coverage (R03 CHA₂DS₂-VASc, R16/R20 safer alternatives); R09 improved to PASS.
- **Stage 2 — citation-truth, 18 usable answers** (`citation_truth_20260614_113716.json`): PMID existence 80/80 (100%, **no fabricated PMIDs**); `has_hallucination` 0/20; mean source_support 90.6. Claim-support over 180 pairs: 45.6% supported / 50% partial / 3.9% unsupported / **0.6% contradicts (1)** / 0% wrong_scope. The 1 CONTRADICTS = polypharmacy/mortality reversal (answer "increased death risk" vs abstract OR 0.78 LOWER, PMID 35268461). B07 = confident AF-stroke-prevention answer with **0 citations**. **Lower-bound** (abstract-only judging over-counts unsupported).
- **Stage 3 — adversarial contradiction, 19 queries** (`adversarial_contradiction_20260614_232655.json`): famous-set reversal 3/16 = **18.8%** (C01 polypharmacy, C03 intensive-glucose, C15 DBP J-curve); cohort-specific 0/3 (underpowered, NOT evidence of safety); 4/186 pairs. C01 reproduced 3× ("reverts counterintuitive finding to intuition"). **Upper-bound** (loaded toward failure). So the true rate sits between ~0.6% (random) and ~19% (adversarial).

**Medical-advisor review:** REJECTED direct B2C public launch in the current state — the "reverses a counterintuitive finding *and* cites a real source" failure is clinically + legally unacceptable (TW 醫師法 §28 密醫罪, FDA device exposure). Recommended **3-phase roadmap**: **Phase 1 (≤1mo)** defensive pivot to B2B (pause B2C, reposition as clinician/pharmacist/researcher literature tool, HCP credential verification at signup, algorithm-oversight committee) → FDA-2026 non-device CDS exemption + avoids 密醫罪; **Phase 2 (3–6mo)** contradiction circuit-breaker + HealthBench-DDI ≥99% + HIPAA-2026 / ONC HTI-1; **Phase 3** SaMD registration (TFDA Class 2 + FDA 510(k)/De Novo) before gradual B2C.

**Prompt-fix attempt + re-measurement (2026-06-15, commit `96806b5`):** the direction-of-effect fidelity instruction (a 3-bullet block in `generator.py`) was applied and re-measured across 7 adversarial runs (2 baseline + 3 post-fix + the prior external baseline + a B01 Stage-2 recheck). **Verdict: PARTIAL mitigation — do NOT read it as "the rate dropped."** Post-fix adversarial famous-set reversal was **19% / 25% / 19%**, INSIDE the baseline band **19% / 25% / 38%** (overlapping; variance-dominated). What genuinely improved: the two stable, reproducible **NEUTRAL-FRAMED** reversers **C01** (polypharmacy/AF) and **C13** (smoker's-paradox STEMI) resolved to faithful (`same`) in all 3 post-fix runs — C01 stayed faithful even when retrieval cited a different PMID (35155632 vs 35268461), so the effect-statistic anchor is a general mechanism, not memorization (live probe showed "lower odds, OR 0.78"). **KEY NEW FINDING — FRAMING-SENSITIVITY:** on the loaded-premise Stage-2 query B01 ("*Why is polypharmacy a serious problem…*"), PMID 35268461 **STILL CONTRADICTS** under the fix — the premise overrides the instruction. Neutral framing fixed; loaded framing NOT — and real B2C users ask loaded questions. Also: C15 (conflicting-evidence + unstable retrieval) did not move; a mild `not_directional` drift (~5.3→~8.7) on non-targeted famous cases to watch; holdout held 0/5 in all 3 runs (no over-correction — but "no new harm" ≠ generalization to unseen reversal modes).

**Model-swap attempt + audit (2026-06-15, o4-mini via the §2.1 Provider abstraction; DONE — NEGATIVE):** swapping the Research generation model gpt-4.1 → o4-mini (reasoning model; needed the provider param-contract fix `455bcb5` to run) did **NOT** fix loaded-framing reversal. The initial o4-mini B01 read was counts-only ("3/3 no-contradict") and **did NOT reproduce**; a **persisted, full-prose** re-run (`tests/results/recheck_20260615_225242.json`, judge gpt-4.1) gave **0/3 fidelity**: run2 + run3 **CONTRADICTS** PMID 35268461 (asserting higher all-cause death "OR 1.82" while citing the OR-0.78-*lower* source — conflated the multimorbidity ORs onto polypharmacy); run1's "no-contradict" was an **evasion** (routed risk through multimorbidity, judge PARTIAL), not a faithful statement of OR 0.78. **No run stated the OR-0.78 lower-death finding.** **Framing-sensitivity is model-swap-resistant** — the SAME o4-mini, SAME PMID, on the NEUTRAL query (C01) stated *"counterintuitively linked to lower odds of all-cause death (OR 0.78)"* faithfully (even flagging the paradox) and attributed other cohorts' higher-mortality HRs to their sources; on the LOADED query (B01) it reversed 2/3. **Only framing differs.** **Scope caveat:** ONE loaded query (B01), 2/3 — the same small-sample limit as the prior optimistic read; not generalized. A GPT-5.4 comparison is an optional last check with LOW expected value (the failure looks systemic, not compute-limited).

**STRUCTURAL FINDING — the reversal problem is TWO distinct failure modes (2026-06-16; Phase-3 eval `evalrun_{o4-mini,gpt-4.1}_20260616` + N=10 retrieval-recall diagnostic `retrieval_recall_20260616_133331.json`, committed `4248dfd`):** this overturns the implicit "bad answers all come from generation" assumption. **(1) GENERATION-reversal (polypharmacy 35268461):** the protective OR-0.78 source is **reliably retrieved at loaded framing** (10/10 in pool AND final at L1/L2, rank 2, never reranked out) — yet the model still states the reversed direction. So the B01 reversals happened **with the source in hand** → a generation failure. A post-hoc direction (NLI) checker is the matching defense. **(2) RETRIEVAL-recall failure (obesity 37290898):** the protective paradox source is **often never retrieved** — loaded OB-L2 = **0/10 in pool** (recall-miss: the loaded rewrite → harm-mechanism/HFpEF papers, paradox doc never enters); neutral OB-L0 = in pool 7/10 but **reranked OUT 7/10** (enters low-scored ~rank 14, reranker keeps 5 newer higher-scored papers). The model **literally never sees** the paradox source and answers from harm-side papers → **a direction-checker CANNOT catch this** (no source in the answer's citations to compare against). Needs retrieval-side fixes. **Sub-findings:** neither anchor is in the local 690-doc store (100% FDA labels) — both are live-PubMed + LLM-rewrite, NOT local indexing. Framing biases retrieval itself, **oppositely by topic**: polypharmacy loaded recalls MORE (L2 10/10 vs L0 5/10), obesity loaded SUPPRESSES (L2 0/10 vs L0 7/10) — for obesity, framing and retrieval are the SAME root cause. **Hypothesis (unverified):** anchor title wording (polypharmacy paper = "Multimorbidity and Polypharmacy… Clinical Outcomes" matches loaded harm terms; obesity paper = neutral "Body mass index and survival" → loaded rewrite drifts off it). o4-mini vs gpt-4.1 on clean anchor-retrieved cases tied 3/6 each (same 3) — model-swap still doesn't fix generation-reversal. **Caveat:** single query-wording per topic, N=10, 2 topics — strong directional signal, not generalized; title-driver is a hypothesis.

**(i) RETRIEVAL prompt-fix attempt — FAILED + reverted (2026-06-16; `retrieval_recall_20260616_141620.json` vs `_133331.json`; retriever.py back at HEAD, NO commit):** the first retrieval-stabilization lever — a prompt-level **valence-neutral query-rewrite** instruction (strip the loaded premise from `_rewrite_query`) — failed both gates. **Step A (target):** OB-L2 anchor recall **0/10 → 0/10, no movement** — root cause is NOT valence but a **TERMINOLOGY mismatch**: the rewrite emits the lay term "obesity" while the anchor 37290898's title is "*Body mass index and survival*", so PubMed never matches it (neutralizing the premise can't fix obesity↔BMI). **Step B (regression guard) TRIPPED HARD:** **P-L1 collapsed 10/10 → 0/10** (deterministic, prompt-caused not variance — the neutralized rewrite shifted terms enough to lose a previously-reliable anchor), OB-L0 pool 7/10 → 0/10 — while P-L0 *improved* 5→10 in the SAME edit. **KEY LESSON:** a prompt-level "be neutral" instruction is **too BLUNT a lever** — anchor recall is highly sensitive to the precise search-term string, so a global rewrite instruction perturbs every query's terms unpredictably (fixes one, breaks another); there is **no single global rewrite instruction that simultaneously optimizes recall across topics** (P-L0 up while P-L1 down under the same edit proves it). **Caveat:** single query-wording per topic, N=10, 2 topics — directional, not a generalized "retrieval is unfixable" claim; the obesity↔BMI terminology cause is established for this anchor, the broader pattern is a hypothesis.

**③ DIRECTION-CHECKER prototype (the generation-side defense, BACKLOG §706a (b)) — 3 rounds (2026-06-16; cheap gpt-4.1-mini + factor-focused prompt; persisted answers `recheck_20260615_225242.json` + adversarial JSONs):** **the CORE (direction comparison) is validated; the WRAPPER (decomposition + aggregation) is where the risk lives.** **Round 1+2 (synthetic, single sentences):** detection 4/4 then 8/8 on known reversals, **0 false-positive on clean faithful**, conflation-trap 4/4 cross-topic (it correctly compares the claim's factor to *that factor's* direction even when the abstract reports both directions), terse abstracts 6/6 — so the cheap checker's direction logic generalizes. **Round 3 (REAL messy multi-claim answers, decompose→anchor→aggregate, polypharmacy):** detection **3/4 (one FALSE NEGATIVE)**, FP **1/2** — and every failure is in the WRAPPER, not the direction logic: **(1) decomposition-anchoring FN [the gating risk]** — B01-run2's real reversal slipped through because the decomposer extracted the claim with **empty cited_pmids** (nothing to compare); the *identical* claim in B01-run3 was caught because the decomposer happened to propagate the PMID — same reversal, opposite outcome, **false safety**; **(2) aggregation over-flag FP** — C01-115632 (faithful, correctly said OR 0.78 lower) flagged on a *different* sub-claim ("polypharmacy increases composite thrombotic events") that contradicts one cited source (32509316 null on stroke/SE) while agreeing with another (36495662) → the "ANY claim contradicts ANY source → flag" rule is too aggressive; **(3) over-splitting** (13–21 atomic claims/answer). **NEXT to evaluate — Path b-coarse:** a **WHOLE-ANSWER vs ANCHOR** direction comparison (compare the answer's overall directional stance on the key factor against the protective anchor's effect statistic directly, **bypassing per-claim decomposition/anchoring**) — hypothesis: catches the B01-run2 reversal the per-claim version missed. **Caveats:** one topic (polypharmacy), 7 real answers; the FP + VAGUE flag are debatable sub-claim tensions (so 1/2 FP is a ceiling — the protective-reversal itself was never falsely flagged); the tooling needed a mid-run fix (first attempt VOID — decomposition truncated at max_tokens=600). **Round 4 (Path b-coarse — whole-answer vs anchor, no decomposition):** fixes the gating FN — caught B01-run2 (the reversal per-claim missed) and all 4 real reversals (**detection 4/4**); its 1 FP is a genuinely-mixed answer (leads "higher HR 1.36-1.84", footnotes OR 0.78) so the flag is defensible; trade-off = coarse only sees the main-factor-vs-one-anchor reversal (blind to secondary-factor reversals) and needs an anchor-selection step. **Round 5 (anchor-selection validation — the last gating risk):** **naive selection ("the source the answer's main claim is based on") is CIRCULAR + whitewashing** — 0/3 on the confusion cases (answers citing BOTH protective 35268461-lower AND a higher source), it picks the higher source agreeing with the answer's reversed claim → whitewashes the reversal into a FALSE NEGATIVE (all wrong picks opposite-direction; this would gut the 4/4). The circularity DEFAULTS TO WHITEWASH (auto-finds the source that makes a reversed answer look fine). **ESCAPE (non-circular):** select by **COUNTERINTUITIVENESS-vs-clinical-prior** (which cited source reports the most surprising finding vs common medical assumption, judged by the source's OWN finding + the model's prior, INDEPENDENT of the answer's claim) — recovered **3/3** on the confusion cases; the prior is used only for selection, the direction judgment still comes from answer-vs-source → no circularity. **FOOTGUN:** requires **FULL abstracts** — with 700-char truncation it was 0/3 (the OR-0.78 sentence is buried deep in a stat-dense abstract that leads with the higher finding); full abstracts → 3/3. **VERDICT: coarse ③ is VIABLE but only under (counterintuitiveness-selection + full-abstract), NEVER naive selection.** **Open deps to pin before production:** (a) cross-topic prior reliability; (b) answers citing multiple competing counterintuitive sources (untested). **(a) DONE — VALIDATED 2026-06-17 (`anchor_prior_crosstab_20260617.json`, 6 topics across crisp/paradox/fuzzy bins, cheap gpt-4.1-mini):** counterintuitiveness-selection picked the correct anchor **5/5 on all confusion topics** (incl. 2 crisp paradoxes — obesity, ACCORD inverted-direction — and 2 fuzzy — HRT, alcohol); **0 mis-picks.** The bare prior was correct 5/6 (the one miss = HRT, a debatable GT-labeling artifact) — but **selection was MORE robust than the prior** (it reads the source's explicit effect size, so the one prior-miss still selected right). **A confidence gate is NOT the answer:** zero selection failures to gate, and confidence wouldn't discriminate anyway (the shaky HRT prior was high-confidence 0.8; the most-contested topic alcohol correctly drew the lowest conf 0.7). **Verdict: reliable as-is in this test; residual risk = a prior-miss that propagates to a selection error (not observed) — partly stressed by dep (b).** Caveats: 6 topics, synthetic answers (only polypharmacy real), one model.

**GENERATION-stage question-neutralization probe (2026-06-17, `neutralization_generation_20260617_105516.json`; polypharmacy/35268461 ONLY — proven retrieved, so retrieval is NOT a variable; RETRIEVE-ONCE-FREEZE-CONTEXT, generation-stage only, retrieval query untouched — NOT a repeat of the failed (i)):** decides the ③ division of labor. Retrieved once with the loaded B01 query, **35268461 confirmed in the frozen context**, then generated 4 phrasings × N=5 against the IDENTICAL frozen docs (current gpt-4.1 generator incl. fix `96806b5`), gpt-4.1 fidelity judge. **Result: loaded→neutral reversal 3/10 → 0/10 — neutralizing only the generator's question ELIMINATES the reversal** (neutral arm 10/10 faithful, both neutral phrasings agree → robust across 2 neutral phrasings on polypharmacy (single topic; cross-topic neutralization untested)). **In-run loaded control reproduced reversal (3/10)** → the fix did NOT fully solve it (control valid). **KEY NUANCE — loaded reversal is WORDING-SPECIFIC:** the two loaded phrasings DISAGREE — L1 (B01's vague "serious problem in elderly Asian patients") reverses 3/5, but L2 ("why does polypharmacy *worsen survival*") is **faithful 5/5** (the more-explicit premise self-corrected with OR 0.78). So "loaded→reversal" is not uniform — only the vague-harm-premise wording triggers it. **VERDICT (bin 1 — reversal ~eliminated under neutral): framing (the loaded premise) is the MAIN cause of the reversals that occur → front-end question-neutralization = a cheap effective pre-filter, ③ = a thin backstop; dep (b) pass-threshold = MEDIUM (the neutralization relaxation applies to the framing-induced-reversal path ONLY; dep (b) measures the source-multiplicity path, which front-end neutralization does NOT address — so the relaxation does NOT transfer). dep (b) subsequently PASSED at medium (4/4 correct-pick, see below).** Caveats: single topic, N=5/variant, one generator, 1 frozen context, synthetic neutralization phrasings; conditional on the anchor being retrieved (proven polypharmacy, unknown elsewhere); directional division-of-labor signal, not a production metric.

**dep (b) — competing counterintuitive sources, MEDIUM threshold — DONE/PASS (2026-06-17, `dep_b_competing_sources_20260617.json`):** the last untested selection stressor — when an answer cites TWO opposite-direction counterintuitive sources, does counterintuitiveness-selection still pick the right anchor (the source-multiplicity path, which front-end neutralization does NOT address). 4 two-source bundles (1 real polypharmacy reversal + 3 cross-topic synthetic, full abstracts): **(a) correct-pick 4/4, (b) whitewash 0, (c) fail-safe 0 → MEDIUM PASS** (the bar = pick right OR fail safe; achieved by picking right every time). It handled the two hardest: **glucose** (counterintuitive anchor is the *higher*-mortality ACCORD, INVERTED vs the others — still correct) and **HRT** (genuinely fuzzy-prior competing) — it reads each source's explicit effect size + prior, so a competing opposite-direction source did NOT confuse it. **Residual (carry):** the **fail-safe (abstain/low-conf) leg was NEVER exercised** (selection was accurate) → unproven; confidence was uniformly 0.9 even on contested HRT (the existing calibration debt persists → the fail-safe backstop is unreliable IF a future competing case stumps the selector). Caveats: synthetic answers except polypharmacy, 4 bundles, one model, single answer/bundle. **With dep (b) passing, selection ACCURACY is validated (dep a 5/5 + dep b 4/4). BUT the MEDIUM-threshold fail-safe leg (selector abstains/low-confidence when it cannot cleanly pick) was NEVER exercised — selection was accurate every time — and dep (a)+(b) both show the selector is OVERCONFIDENT on contested topics (uniform 0.9, wrong-but-0.8 on HRT). So the fail-safe safety net is UNVALIDATED and likely UNRELIABLE. DESIGN CONSTRAINT for production ③ (carry into the build spec): the flag/withhold trigger must NOT depend on the selector's self-reported confidence (it won't abstain — it will mis-pick confidently = the dangerous whitewash mode). Instead, trigger flag STRUCTURALLY — detect the *situation* 'answer cites ≥2 opposite-direction counterintuitive sources on the same factor→outcome' and flag unconditionally, regardless of selector confidence. Safety rests on situation-detection, not on the selector grading itself. What's left is PRODUCTION BUILD (engineering): the front-end vague-loaded-neutralization mechanism (unbuilt, §706a (b2) gap) + coarse ③ integration (unbuilt).**

**C1 (③) SHADOW BUILD — BUILT + VALIDATED → FAILED real-data shadow validation (2026-06-17, `direction_shadow_20260617_143022.json` + `…_rescore_nostruct_151126.json`; plan-back `C1_inrepo_planback.md`; code committed locally, NOT pushed, Fly deploy DEFERRED):** first prod-code touch in this chain. Gate 1 (credit probe through the real provider path) HEALTHY; Gate 2 push = no-op (`96806b5`/`fdd624c` already on origin/main); Fly deploy deferred (founder) — baseline captured in-process on HEAD (gpt-4.1, `96806b5` live). Built: `api/services/direction_checker.py` (async port of the validated core, 4 invariants), `server.py` shadow hook `_run_direction_check_background` behind `DIRECTION_CHECK_SHADOW` (default OFF, mirrors `_run_judge_background`, touches no SSE/answer), `scripts/direction_shadow_eval.py` (+ `_rescore`). Corpus frozen pre-generation (`tests/direction_shadow_corpus.json`: full 127 golden + 24 adversarial; live subset 43 = 25 targeted + 18 clean-faithful, clean criterion frozen first). **REAL NUMBERS — C1 as-built is NOT deployable (do NOT enable the flag): structural-ON detection 11/11 but FP 12/16 (75%), flags 37/43 (86% of ALL answers); structural-OFF (primary whole-answer-vs-anchor only) detection 0/11, FP 1/16.** The "11/11" is an ARTIFACT of the structural trigger rubber-stamping `two_opposite_counterintuitive=True` on real 4–5-source retrievals (35/43 verdict=structural, incl. unambiguous single-direction topics — peanut-LEAP, beta-blockers-HFrEF, digoxin, serotonin-syndrome) → no precision. The primary path detects NOTHING because **C1's own cheap anchor-scorer fell into the exact B01 conflation trap it exists to catch** (P-L1: read the multimorbidity OR 2.04 instead of polypharmacy OR 0.78 from the multi-stat abstract) + the deterministic `_opposite` can't handle "mixed" anchors (OB-L1). **VERDICT: the dep(a) 5/5 / dep(b) 4/4 / coarse 4/4 feasibility validations used hand-curated synthetic bundles + curated answers and did NOT generalize to the real pipeline — on real retrievals C1 is unusable in BOTH configs (structural = no precision; primary = no recall). C1 needs FUNDAMENTAL REWORK, not tuning.** Shadow-first caught it before any user exposure (the build's purpose). Caveats: 43 cases, 1 generator, 1 run; FP denom (16) is direction-ADJACENT (C1's mis-fire rate on the answers it acts on), NOT a site-wide rate. → TECH_DEBT (new C1 entry) + BACKLOG §706a (b)/(c).

**C1 follow-up DIAGNOSTIC — conflation is a CHEAP-MODEL artifact, NOT task-intrinsic (2026-06-17, `scripts/direction_extract_probe.py`, `direction_extract_probe_20260617_153856.json`; route now B2B-interim so advisory, not launch-gating; $0.022, 5 cases, no regeneration):** isolated re-run of ONLY the two ③ sub-tasks with a STRONGER model (gpt-4.1, not mini) on captured answers. **(a) extraction:** gpt-4.1 cleanly disentangled the multi-stat conflation trap — P-L1 + P-L2 both got polypharmacy `OR 0.78 (decreases)` AND named the `multimorbidity OR 2.04 (increases)` trap (2/2 — the exact case the mini scorer failed); clean controls 2/2. **(b) LLM comparator** (replacing deterministic `_opposite`): detection 2/2 on conflation reversals, FP 0/2 on clean. **VERDICT: C1's primary-path failure = cheap scorer + brittle comparator, NOT the task → ③ HAS a path as a thin B2B backstop IF built with a strong scorer (gpt-4.1, cents/answer) + LLM comparator.** STILL UNRESOLVED (don't over-read 2/2): the structural over-fire (75% FP) is model-independent and must be DROPPED; anchor-SELECTION over real retrievals untested (probe fed the known anchor); single-source "mixed"/U-shaped abstracts still oversimplified even by gpt-4.1 (OB-L1 dropped the U-shaped caveat). So ③ is not dead, but "viable" is CONDITIONAL on drop-structural + prove-selection-at-scale + handle-mixed.

**RETRIEVAL-RECALL REFUSAL lever — SHADOW DETECTION BUILT + VALIDATED → PASSES (2026-06-18, `retrieval_refusal_20260618_095609.json` + `_prior_probe_095754.json`; plan-back `retrieval_refusal_planback.md`; code committed locally, NOT pushed, Fly deploy deferred, flag default OFF):** covers the failure ③/C1 is STRUCTURALLY BLIND to — a confident answer where the protective/counter source was NEVER retrieved (the obesity-type retrieval-miss; the worst-case that is LIVE now for walk-in users). Route is B2B-interim; this is the advisor's "if the pool is one-directional, refuse to advise without counter-evidence" circuit-breaker + future SaMD defense arch. Gate 1 HEALTHY. **Design = H1 (POOL-STANCE-SPREAD × CONTESTED-PRIOR): refuse = (pool one-sided on factor→outcome) AND (a counter-direction is genuinely contested in CURRENT literature).** Built: `api/services/retrieval_refusal.py` (async, gpt-4.1 — mini conflates), `server.py` shadow hook `_run_retrieval_refusal_background` behind `RETRIEVAL_REFUSAL_SHADOW` (default OFF, mirrors the C1 hook, touches no SSE/answer), `scripts/retrieval_refusal_eval.py` + `_prior_probe.py`. Corpus frozen pre-run (`tests/retrieval_refusal_corpus.json`: detection OB-L0/1/2 + complete-pool P-L1/2 + over-refusal control n=9 incl. counterintuitive-but-SETTLED stress cases; criterion frozen first). Reused captured pools (no re-retrieval). Reads the FINAL pool the generator saw (`documents`). Mid-build refinement: the per-source read is STANCE/valence (harmful/beneficial/mixed), not strict mortality-HR — a harm-framed mechanism review counts as harmful (spot-checked: OB-L2 run2's `beneficial` reads are REAL paradox mentions). **REAL NUMBERS — the OPPOSITE of C1: DETECTION 3/3 retrieval-miss cases fired (by run 7/9; the 2 non-fires correctly had paradox-conveying pools); OVER-REFUSAL 0/9 (with 8/9 one-sided pools = the prior leg genuinely stressed); complete-pool 0/2 over-fire; PRIOR generalization 10/10 contested-vs-settled across diverse topics (glucose-ACCORD/DBP/alcohol/sodium/HRT/obesity True; peanut/BB-HFrEF/smoking/statins False); cost $0.16.** The C1 "flag everything" failure did NOT recur — the prior leg separates genuinely-contested (obesity paradox → refuse) from counterintuitive-but-settled (CAST/LEAP/smoking-PD → don't refuse). H2/H3 NOT needed. **Caveats (do NOT over-read):** end-to-end DETECTION validated on ONE contested topic (obesity, 3 framings — the only retrieval-miss topic captured); prior generalizes 10/10 but other contested topics' live pools firing is untested end-to-end (needs live retrieval); the prior rests on gpt-4.1 world-knowledge (a knowledge dependency — H3 registry could backstop); reused pools, single run, n=9/n=6 modest; per-query firing is pool-composition-dependent (probabilistic, not deterministic); SHADOW DETECTION only (enforcement = A2/route-gated). → BACKLOG (broaden contested-topic detection on live retrieval before enabling). Flag stays OFF.

**LEVER 2 — FRONT-END QUESTION-NEUTRALIZATION — A/B SHADOW BUILT + VALIDATED → SAFE + EFFECTIVE-WHERE-REVERSAL-OCCURS, but NARROW (NOT a cross-topic pass) (2026-06-18, `question_neutralization_20260618_110411.json` + de-risk `_104531.json`; plan-back `question_neutralization_planback.md`; committed locally, NOT pushed, flag default OFF):** the OTHER failure mode (vs Lever 1) — GENERATION-reversal, source-in-hand: protective source retrieved but a LOADED framing makes the generator state the direction backwards. Lever rewrites the INTERNAL generator question to neutral (retrieval + user-facing question untouched). NOT a pure-observer shadow — it changes the answer, so the shadow is an **A/B harness** (both arms on the SAME frozen context, retrieve-once-freeze). Gate 1 HEALTHY. Built: `api/services/question_neutralization.py` (`is_loaded` detector + intent/qualifier-preserving `neutralize`), `server.py` flag-gated rewrite behind `QUESTION_NEUTRALIZATION_SHADOW` (default OFF, fail-open), `scripts/question_neutralization_eval.py`, corpus `tests/question_neutralization_corpus.json` (5 source-in-hand topics, vague-loaded framings + clean control n=8). Design = **detect-then-neutralize** (confirmed: detector 0/16 over-trigger on clean → safe to gate). **REAL NUMBERS — per topic (loaded→neutral REVERSED, /10): polypharmacy 4/10 → 0/10 (ELIMINATED ✅); beta_blockers_hfref 0/10→0/10; beta_carotene 0/10→0/10; cast 0/10→0/10; peanut retrieval-miss.** **HONEST VERDICT: reversal-reduction demonstrated on POLYPHARMACY ONLY** — the other 3 source-in-hand topics do NOT reverse under loaded framing (verified REAL faithful answers, not judge misses: BB "cornerstone… lower mortality HR 0.66"; beta-carotene "RCTs showed harm"). Per the founder's bar, only-polypharmacy ≠ cross-topic pass. The limiting factor is NOT the lever — **source-in-hand loaded-reversal is a NARROW phenomenon largely confined to the conflation-trap structure** (multimorbidity OR 1.82/2.04 co-located with polypharmacy OR 0.78 → vague-loaded framing grabs the harm stat); clean single-direction topics stay faithful. **OVER-NEUTRALIZATION (gating risk) PASSES strongly:** detector over-trigger 0/16, intent/qualifier-retention failures 0/16 (every clinical qualifier preserved), 0 REAL clean-regressions (both 1/8 "regressions" are generation-stochasticity artifacts — CLN-5's neutralized question was IDENTICAL to the original). Cost $1.11 + $0.45. **Net: the lever is SAFE + eliminates reversal where it occurs, but its addressable surface is narrow.** Two-lever coverage = Lever 1 (retrieval-miss, obesity-type, PASS) + Lever 2 (source-in-hand loaded reversal, polypharmacy-type, narrow). Caveats: 4 source-in-hand topics, 2 framings/topic, N=5, single frozen context/topic (reversal is pool-dependent). Flag stays OFF; A2/enforcement route-gated. → BACKLOG.

**DECISION STATE — OPEN (not decided):** founder wants BOTH B2C + B2B viable and agreed to "fix the most serious problems first" rather than pause B2C outright. The route (B2C-after-fix vs B2B-interim) is **PENDING**. **Update (2026-06-15): BOTH "reduce-bad-generation" paths are tried-and-insufficient for B2C** — the prompt-fix (PARTIAL; loaded-framing persists) AND the o4-mini model-swap (0/3 fidelity on B01-loaded). **Update (2026-06-16 #1): the defense roadmap must cover BOTH failure modes** (generation-reversal + retrieval-recall failure); ③ post-hoc checker is **blind** to the retrieval half. **Update (2026-06-16 #2): the retrieval-PROMPT-fix lever is now tried-and-failed (see above) → direction changes.** The retrieval-recall failure (obesity-type) is **hard to fix at the retrieval layer** (deeper levers = lay→MeSH term-expansion or two-sided retrieval = "Path B, deferred"), so it shifts to being **ABSORBED by ① structural-citation fallback** (detect missing protective coverage → low-confidence/withhold/flag, rather than guaranteeing retrieval). **NEW PRIMARY BUILD FOCUS = the GENERATION-reversal half** (polypharmacy-type, source-in-hand): ③ post-hoc direction checker + structural-citation binding (§706a (b)+(c)) — the confidently-actionable half. **Net roadmap: (C) generation-side defenses (③ / structural binding) + (A) ① fallback to absorb retrieval-miss; (B) deeper retrieval-stabilization deferred.** This **strengthens (does not decide) the lean toward "B2B-interim first; B2C gated on defensive mechanisms, not on model choice."** PREREQUISITE for any further eval run: OpenAI auto-recharge ON / balance sufficient (TECH_DEBT P0 ops).

**Framing discipline (keep in all docs):** the QA is EVIDENCE, not an endorsement; the unsupported/contradicts rate is a **lower bound** (abstract-only); the adversarial rate is an **upper bound** (loaded). NLI/contradiction-gate feasibility was assessed (NOT built) — verdict PARTIAL-not-reliable (see TECH_DEBT). OpenAI auto-recharge is OFF + prepaid credit expires → credit-zero returns `[ERROR]` to all users, a launch-gating stability risk (TECH_DEBT).

Commits: tech-debt cleanup (`e555b41` — drift-to-done doc pruning + dead-file/key removal [deleted `api/data_sources/fda_cached.py` + `components/Untitled` + dead i18n keys] + restored `npm run lint` via a new `eslint.config.mjs` + `next.config.ts eslint.ignoreDuringBuilds`) · blog-spec tracking (`564f518`) · **citation-original-language note** (`6796c08`) · **DailyMed over-claim copy fix + deferred-item records** (`ead65fb`) · v188 bookkeeping (`d602a0a`). **Two USER-VISIBLE changes now LIVE + human-verified on prod:** (1) the **citation-language note** — a small grey caption on non-English Research citation panels (zh-TW「文獻保留原文,避免翻譯造成失真。」) explaining references stay in their source language, shown only when `lang!=='en' && citations.length>0` (frontend-only; `citationLanguageNote` i18n key ×16; 6 review-gate locales th/ar/hi/bn/he/vi still pending native check); (2) the **corrected DailyMed attribution copy** — the 6 Bucket-A footer/FAQ/composer strings no longer claim data "via DailyMed", now say "FDA drug labels (OpenFDA)" (the functional DailyMed *lookup* links + FDA tooltip were KEPT — those are verification tools, not source-claims). **Bundle-verified in the deployed JS:** "FDA official drug labels (OpenFDA)" present, old "official drug labels via" claim GONE (0), "文獻保留原文" present, `dailymed.nlm.nih.gov` Bucket-B links still present. **Lever config UNCHANGED** (`RETRIEVAL_REFUSAL_SHADOW` digest `d8c5ac2e11c8e492` = known-good `d8c5ac2e`; `DIRECTION_CHECK_SHADOW`/C1 + `QUESTION_NEUTRALIZATION_SHADOW`/Lever 2 absent/OFF; `NEXT_PUBLIC_LOCALE_HINT_ENABLED` still absent → locale-hint OFF; OpenAI auto-recharge ON). What it is: when a zh-TW Research answer touches a Taiwan local-difference dimension (dosing / NHI reimbursement / indication / contraindication), a frontend panel NAMES the dimension(s) + POINTS to TFDA/NHI for the user to verify — asserts no clinical fact, states no local value, explicit it has NOT integrated TFDA/NHI. **Dogfood-validated:** deterministic keyword trigger (NO LLM classifier needed) + frontend-rendered panel + **no-retrieval (`isFallback`) suppression** (panel hidden on ungrounded "基於一般醫學知識" answers) + **precision-tuned** (narrowed dosing/contraindication so it fires on real local-difference dimensions, not generic boilerplate). **NEW build-time flag `NEXT_PUBLIC_LOCALE_HINT_ENABLED`** is the switch for the eventual "enable for all zh-TW" step (requires the full human-eye gate first). Frontend-only across all three (only `utils/localeHint.ts`, `components/LocaleHintPanel.tsx`, `utils/i18n-ui.ts`, `pages/research.tsx`); flag config UNCHANGED (`RETRIEVAL_REFUSAL_SHADOW=true` same digest `d8c5ac2e`, C1 + Lever 2 OFF — no backend touched). ### 🔗 BLOCKING CHAIN — the long-lead-time human dependency

```
Generative UI LAYER 2  ←  §5.1 六國 moat  ←  b2 (JP/KR/TH Tier-1 data)  ←  ja / ko / th NATIVE CLINICAL REVIEW
                                                                              ↑ HUMAN dependency, longest lead time
```

- **b2's blocker is NOT code.** `resolveCountry` already returns JP/KR/TH and RULE 2 falls them to Tier-2, so **b2 is pure data work** — the block is **linguistic review of authority strings, which must not be machine-translated** (the rule that produced NPRA's confirmed Malay name rather than the model's first guess).

  > **① THE DEFECT (recon §5.1, confirmed Phase 0 from code + history + prod evidence):** real Dodo payloads carry neither `data.id` nor a top-level `id` (top-level keys: `business_id`/`data`/`timestamp`/`type`; `data` has `subscription_id`), so the payload-derived key ALWAYS fell through to a locally-generated fallback — epoch-ms in `32a82a4`, uuid4 after `4443855` — and every replay minted a fresh key: **`already_processed` could never fire**. Prod's `dodo_dodo_<epoch-ms>` rows are the residue (double prefix = the fallback's own `dodo_` wrapped by the storage sites' f-string).   > **④ A8 — WHAT IT FINDS ON THE LIVE LEDGER TODAY (reported, NOT repaired):** STATE **8 WRONG / 29 drifted** · TECH_DEBT **7 / 28** · BACKLOG **2 / 10** · CLAUDE **0 / 1**. Real hits include `pages/history.tsx:337` (file has **327** lines), `api/data_sources/fda_cached.py` (**Rule 8 says it is dead code** — the citation outlives the file), `tests/test_faq_theme_tokens.py` (renamed in the theme car), and `services/cost_tracker.py` / `app/data/tfda/brand_ingredient.json` (**wrong path prefixes**). **This car does not fix any of them** — the instrument proving itself is not a repair car.
  > **⑤ 🔴 THE FINDING THAT MATTERS MORE THAN THE CHECKER — WRONG-SUPPORT, N=1.** The founder-directed human review of the 24-unit pack read **9 scoreable units** and found **zero wrong-drug answers**, including all four deliberately-loaded misfires. But `R12` (*"is it ok to give both blood thinners at the same time"*) surfaced a **different axis**: the answer's clinical scenarios (DOAC + antiplatelet, warfarin + aspirin) rest on **one PubMed citation**, while its **three DailyMed citations — CLOPIDOGREL BISULFATE, TIROFIBAN, UROKINASE — are three different mechanism classes and NOT ONE IS A DRUG PAIR.** Every citation is real, relevant and correctly attributed; **none carries the inference the answer draws.**
  > **🔴 NO GATE MEASURES THIS — checked against each criterion, not assumed.** canary keys on `wrong_object_cited` (identity) · danger-path v1/v3 on source type and clearance · the human-eye form on `EXPECTED vs OBSERVED OWNER` (identity) · §2.7's hard floor on source-type validity and fabrication. ⚠️ **Two partial exceptions, named so the claim is not overstated:** the danger-path's **v2 `leans_on_descriptive`** is support-shaped but scoped to descriptive-vs-safety on 3 queries; and **`citation_truth_check.py` genuinely checks direction-of-effect** — but it is not a gate, is PubMed-only, and 🔴 **its `extract_pmid` mis-extracts the other source types** (measured: a DailyMed id yields PMID **84432**, a TFDA id yields **057803**), so it would judge R12's DailyMed citations against unrelated abstracts. **Flagged, not fixed.**
  > **MECHANISM, and it is not the resolver bug:** `R12` **names no drug at all**, so retrieval can only fetch anticoagulation-*adjacent* documents and the generator organises them into a complete-looking answer. The resolver's exact/base exclusivity needs **both** drugs named; wrong-support needs **neither**. Different shapes, no shared fix. **N=1 — no rate exists and none is claimed.**
  > **⑥ WHAT THE REVIEW CLEARED, since it is real.** Four class queries cited correct class members **without over-generalising single-drug properties to the class** (R07 beta-blockers · R16 NSAIDs · R18 statins · R20 ACE inhibitors); **R16 separated PubMed trial evidence from DailyMed's shared class warning.** The CALCIUM-shaped clinical failure **did not appear**. The four misfires returned **zero DailyMed citations** — a property of the unshipped seat.
  > **⚠️ BOUNDS RECORDED WITH THE RESULT, NOT BENEATH IT:** N=1 per query against **turnover 0.423**; curated and deliberately-loaded population; **95% upper bound ≈ 12%** — a defect hitting 1 in 8 answers is fully consistent with this sweep. **Detector, not estimator.** And **coverage was 22 of 102 citations** — the **80 PubMed citations have no drug-identity check at all.**
  > **⚠️ The pack's VERDICT column is unfilled** — the reviewer's verdicts live in the TECH_DEBT entries, and the `.md` is committed as instrument + evidence. Said plainly so the empty column is not read as "not reviewed".
  > **VERIFICATION:** `git diff --stat -- api/ components/ pages/` **EMPTY** · `pytest` **316 / 28 UNCHANGED** · the seven content anchors each still `grep -c` to **1** · nav **RE-DERIVED**: **0 + 8 + 17 + 42 + 79 = 146** ([HONESTY] 16→17, [DONE] 41→42).
